**`WF-17` step 1: a user-guide page whose "first study" block is the test input.** One new page, `docs/guide/first_study.md`, carries one fenced block tagged ` ```python first-study `. A new test module reads the **committed page**, extracts that block verbatim and `exec`s it through the harness at `-n 2`, degree 2 by default. The mean SAR it returns must reproduce `WF-15`'s record inside `L0_BAND`, and land inside `MAT-4`'s imported band against the closed form. `docrefs` gains an additive, opt-in Markdown-link pass and reports zero dead links over `docs/guide/`, with a planted dead link detected.
- **Executor:** implementer.
- **Tier:**
  - W0 / W0b smoke (real build, no `mpiexec`);
  - W1 standard (complex, `-n 2`);
  - W2 heavy (complex, `-n 2`, 590 s).

  The item is **heavy, `-n 2`**. The row's "standard by prediction" is re-declared by the price below: one degree-2 solve on this fixture is 282.4 s.
- **Class:** chain step (solve + docs), measured median ≈ 9 min.
- **Cost:** **predicted ≈ 40 min.** That is 15 fixed + ≈ 15 code (page ≈ 60 lines, test ≈ 200 lines, checker ≈ 40 lines) + windows W0 0.5 + W0b 0.1 + W1 1.5 + sweep 0.2 + W2 6 min + ≈ 2 spare.
- **Depends on:** nothing open. `study.py`, the `WF-15` module and its logs are on `main` (4239ba88 and later).

**Settled from the code (read before designing).**
- **`docrefs` (`scripts/testing/check_example_doc_references.py`) is blind to Markdown links today.**
  - Its reference pass matches only filename-shaped tokens ending `.py` or an artifact suffix (`REFERENCE_RE`, `:120–122`; `ARTIFACT_SUFFIXES`, `:116`).
  - It checks a `.py` only by **basename** anywhere under `src/ examples/ scripts/ tests/` (`repo_basenames`, `:266–272`).
  - A `[text](../x.md)` link, a link to a moved directory, or a wrong relative path is never resolved.
  - `--docs-root` already accepts any repo-relative tree (`:366–370`, `:382`), so `--docs-root docs/guide` scans the guide. The guide pass (`:459–480`) runs over `./run_examples.sh --list` whatever the docs root, and its `guide=` count is the examples corpus's, not the guide's.
  - **So the Done-when's "zero dead links" needs an additive link pass.** Without one, the guide-mode `dead=0` is vacuous for links.
- **There is no `docs/guide/` (or `docs/user/`) directory.** `mkdocs.yml` has a nav, but nothing in the harness builds it, and the row does not claim a hosted site. This item **does not edit `mkdocs.yml` or `docs/index.md`** (see open questions).

- (1) **New `docs/guide/first_study.md`**, the user-guide page. Sections:
  - **What you will compute:** mean SAR in a 1 cm-radius gelled-saline sphere under an imposed uniform 1 V/m field at 64 MHz, checked against the closed form.
  - **Before you start:** the Docker service, complex mode `source /usr/local/bin/dolfinx-complex-mode`, and a cost of ≈ 5 min and ≈ 20 GiB summed RAM at 2 ranks (W2a: 282.4 s, 20.41 GiB, `…W2a…:158`).
  - **The first study:** the one tagged block.
  - **Run it:** save the block as a script and run `mpiexec -n 2 python3 first_study.py` inside the container in complex mode.
  - **What you should see:** one fenced ` ```text first-study-output ` block holding `mean SAR = 8.0116577245e-08 W/kg`. The prose says that the last digit or two can differ between runs (≈ 1e-11 relative) and that the test holds it to 1e-9. The closed form is 8.008354e-08 W/kg, 0.04 % away.
  - **What this does not show:** no coil, no ports, no h-convergence, an imposed field only.
  - **How this page is tested:** a relative link to the test module.

  The page links, by relative path, to `../../src/fem_em_solver/study.py`, `../../tests/package/test_wf17_first_study_tutorial.py` and `../../tests/package/test_wf15_study_lossy_sphere.py` (≥ 3 links, all live). It cites **no run artifact** (`.json` / `.png` / `.xdmf` …), so the freshness rule cannot fire on it. Write `≈`, never `~`. No AED number.

  **The block** uses only existing names and the `Study` defaults: **no `degree=` and no `density_kg_m3=`.** To reproduce `GEOMETRY` bitwise it writes `R = 0.01` and `resolution_sphere=R / 10, resolution_far=R / 5`, the same float division as `SPHERE_RADIUS / 10.0`. It also passes `GelledSalinePhantomMaterial(0.57, 78.0, 64e6)`, `QuasiStaticSphereField(e0=1.0)` and `frequency_hz=64e6`. It calls `sar = study.mean_sar()` and prints `f"mean SAR = {sar['mean_sar_w_per_kg']:.10e} W/kg"`. It has no `if __name__` guard requirement: the test execs with `__name__ == "__main__"`, so either form runs.

**Traps already paid for.**
- **Never compare a solve output bitwise or by its printed string.** Two `-n 2` MUMPS solves differ at ≈ 1e-11 across processes (W2b 2.633e-11; known-issues.md:81, `SOLVE_FLOOR_RECORD`), and the `.10e` print of this value sits 4.8e-12 from a rounding edge.
- **Two degree-2 solves never share a 590 s window on this fixture at `-n 2`** (item 240). This item has one per window by construction. Do not add a direct-call comparison to W2.
- **Price a degree-2 window by the fixture's own measurement** (item 240's 1.8× miss): the price above is `WF-15`'s own.
- **`TimeHarmonicSolver(problem)` silently means degree 1** (`core/time_harmonic.py:318`). (Deg) and (K)'s no-`degree=` AST check catch a fall-through.
- **Rule (i) bites harder here than usual: the page is the test's input.** Fixing a typo in the page after W2 invalidates W2, which costs a 6-minute re-run. The sha256 prints make the check auditable from the logs.
- **`exec` and MPI.** The block runs on every rank, because the solve is collective. Capture stdout per rank and echo it from rank 0 only. Never put a `comm.` call under `if comm.rank == 0:`, which deadlocks (carried ruling, 10-04). `redirect_stdout` does not capture C-level gmsh / PETSc output, and that is fine.
- **Restore the monkeypatched `Study` methods** with pytest's `monkeypatch` fixture, never by hand.
- **The guide pass in `docrefs` is the examples corpus's.** Assert `dead` for the guide, never `exit == 0` inside the test, or an unrelated example's missing heading turns `WF-17` red. W0b's bare log records the full `RESULT:` line.
- **Keep the default `docrefs` path byte-identical.** The `example-runner` census reads it on every `EX-*` item. W0's exit-code module (`test_current_tree_is_free_of_hard_violations_and_obeys_the_contract`, the `OPS-42` identity) is the guard.
- **The page cites no run artifact.** An `.json` / `.png` name would enter the freshness rule and could turn `stale=` nonzero over time.

**Code** (names are proposals; the executor may rename and discloses the mapping).
- (1) **New `scripts/docs/generate_api_reference.py`.**
  - `render(name) -> str` builds the page text:
    - a generated-file header comment;
    - `# <name>`;
    - the kind (class / function / exception);
    - the `inspect.signature` text, with any `0x…` address scrubbed;
    - `inspect.getdoc` verbatim in a fenced `text` block (or `*(no docstring)*`);
    - the defining source file as a relative link. Use `inspect.getsourcefile`, relativised to the repo root. **Raise if it is not under the repo**, for example an installed copy (`WF-13`);
    - a `## Validated by` section between `<!-- envelope:begin -->` / `<!-- envelope:end -->` markers. It holds one bullet per registry node, in registry order: the node ID in backticks, plus a relative link to the node's file (the part before `::`);
    - a link to `index.md`.
  - `index.md` lists every name, each linked to its page.
  - `generate(out_dir)` writes every page and the index. A `--check` flag writes to a temporary directory and diffs it against `out_dir`, exiting 1 on any difference.
  - Output is deterministic: sorted names and `\n` line endings.
- (2) **Committed `docs/guide/api/*.md`**, generated by Wgen (35 files today).
- (3) **New `tests/package/test_wf17_api_reference.py`.**

  - **Parsing.** Nodes are parsed **from the committed page text** between the markers. They are never taken from the registry, so the page is what is checked.

    - `test_wf17_pages_are_what_the_generator_emits` (G);
    - `test_wf17_page_envelope_equals_registry` (E);

- **(N) One page per public name.** `{p.stem for p in docs/guide/api/*.md} − {"index"} == set(_surface()) == set(_load_snapshot())`, and the index links each name exactly once. No two names collide case-insensitively (a case-insensitive checkout would silently merge two pages). The page count is printed.
- **(G) The committed pages are the generator's output.** `generate(tmp)` is byte-identical to the committed tree: the same file set and equal bytes per file. A second `generate(tmp2)` is identical to the first, which shows determinism. The first differing file is printed on failure.
- **(E) Each page renders its envelope entry.** For every name, the node list parsed from the page equals `registry[name]["nodes"]`, as an equal list in registry order.
- **(C) Every page's nodes collect.** Run the collect-only subprocess over the union of node files parsed from the pages. Assert `rc == 0` and `_node_violations(page_registry, collected_ids) == []`. Files and ids are printed. This is the same identity `WF-14` (C) asserts, read through the pages.
- **(D) `docrefs` over the guide, pages included.** `run_checker("--docs-root", "docs/guide", "--check-links")` parses `dead == 0`, and the `Link pass:` line reads pages ≥ 36 (34 API pages + index + `first_study.md`) and links ≥ 34 × 3. n and m are printed. `exit` is printed, not asserted (item 257's trap: `guide=` is the examples corpus's).

**Negative controls (rule (e)).** All are *asserted, by construction*: deterministic functions of new code on synthetic input (the `OPS-19` precedent, `test_doc_reference_exit_codes.py:161–178`; item 257 (c1) / (c3)). The review may relabel them as predicted.

A reading of 0 is blind; 2 means fences are not skipped.

**Traps already paid for.**
- **Signature reprs can carry memory addresses** (`<object at 0x7f…>` defaults), which makes (G) flaky between processes. Scrub them in `render`, and (G)'s two-generation identity catches a miss.
- **`inspect.getsourcefile` under an installed copy points into site-packages** (`WF-13`'s installed-location test exists for exactly this). With `PYTHONPATH=/workspace/src` it is `/workspace/src/…`. The generator raises rather than writing a link that is dead on the next box.

- **The surface moves under concurrent items** (25 → 34 in two days). Regenerate after the rebase, and never take `docs/guide/api/` by path from a branch.
- **No pipe or redirect in the harness command; status 124 is a finding, not a retry.** No compute runs, so no orphan sweep is needed.
- **Write `≈`, never `~`, in tracked Markdown** (docstrings are rendered inside fenced blocks, so a `~` there is inert). Quote no AED number. Docstrings carrying one would be a finding: grep the generated pages for `AED` before committing.

**The page** (headings as `four_port_s.md`: `# The tuned copper birdcage`, `## What you will compute`, `## Before you start`, `## The study`, `## Run it`, `## What you should see`, `## What this does not show`, `## How this page is tested`). Prose, from the two examples, quoting none of their numbers beyond the record:
- from `ports:21` (`examples/ports/21_capacitor_esr_sheet.md`): a lumped capacitor with a 0.2 Ω series resistance is a sheet with `Z = r + 1/(jωC)`; what the ESR does to the loss partition;
- from `mri:17` (`examples/mri/17_f_human_tuned_quadrature_b1_sar.md`): the 16-leg copper coil cut out of the air (a Leontovich wall), C_in on all 32 gaps, two feeds 90° apart (P17 / P21), `ccw` = (1, −1j) is the co-rotating sense, and why the feed-bisector mirror is the identity a two-feed drive keeps;
- "Before you start": complex build; **8 MPI ranks, ≈ 3–4 min**; `degree=1` set explicitly (the `Study` default is 2, which this coil cannot afford here).
- **The block** (tag `tuned-copper-birdcage`, exactly one `Study(` call; it writes no file):

  ```python
  import numpy as np

  from fem_em_solver import (
      FHumanBirdcageGeometry,
      GelledSalinePhantomMaterial,
      LeontovichWall,
      Study,
      TwoPortQuadratureDrive,
  )

  geometry = FHumanBirdcageGeometry(as_hole=True)  # copper coil cut out of the air
  phantom = GelledSalinePhantomMaterial(0.5, 78.0, 64e6)  # sigma S/m, eps_r, Hz
  c_in = 118.2437 * 1e-12  # the in-model Larmor capacitance on copper, F
  drive = TwoPortQuadratureDrive(c_in, esr_ohm=0.2)  # 0.2 ohm ESR on every gap

  study = Study(geometry, phantom, drive, frequency_hz=64e6,
                conductor=LeontovichWall(), degree=1)
  az = np.radians(11.25)  # the P17 feed azimuth
  c_star = (0.045 * np.cos(az), 0.045 * np.sin(az), 0.0)  # r = 45 mm, mid-plane
  sar = study.sar(mass_g=10.0, centres=[c_star], drives=("ccw",))
  print(f"SAR10g(c*) = {sar['ccw'][0]['averaged_sar_w_per_kg']:.4e} W/kg")
  ```
  Names follow item 382's landed API; if it landed different names (`esr_ohm` vs the weekly's `esr`, the `drives` key), the executor adapts the block's *names* only and discloses.
- **Output block** (tag `tuned-copper-birdcage-output`, `text`): the single line `SAR10g(c*) = <record at .4e> W/kg`. With the record 4.852895959e-03 it reads `4.8529e-03`.
- **"How this page is tested":** links by full filename (rule (b)) to `tests/package/test_wf17_tuned_copper_tutorial.py` and `tests/package/test_wf16_study_tuned_f_human_b1_sar.py`. "What this does not show": no tuning-accuracy, `f₀`, Q or match claim; no compliance or absolute SAR claim; c\* is a lattice argmax, not a global maximum; degree 1 only; no AED statement.

**The page** (headings as `tuned_copper_birdcage.md`: `# The tuned copper birdcage from a YAML file`, `## What you will compute`, `## Before you start`, `## The study file`, `## Run it`, `## What you should see`, `## What this does not show`, `## How this page is tested`). - "What you will compute": the same coil as `tuned_copper_birdcage.md`, described in a study file instead of Python; `femem run` solves it once and writes a JSON summary (prose, never a file name) with the node S, the active admittances `Y_in` per sense, the 10 g SAR at 19 ball centres and \|B₁⁺\| at two points.
- **The 19th centre (390a's finding, stated, not hidden):** the file's centre 19 is M(c\*), the image of c\* under the feed-bisector mirror (56.25°), which maps az 11.25° onto 101.25° — it coincides bit for bit with lattice row 6 (r = 45 mm, az₁₇ + 90°, index 5), so it is **not an independent centre**; it is listed so the summary carries the `cw` reading at the mirror image beside c\*'s `ccw` reading. The page never presents it as a nineteenth sample.
- "Before you start": complex build; **8 MPI ranks, ≈ 4–5 min**; the file sets `degree: 1` (an omitted `degree` means the `Study` default, 2 — which this coil cannot afford here).
- **The YAML block** (tag `f-human-tuned-copper`, fence ` ```yaml f-human-tuned-copper `): the tracked file's 45 lines verbatim, its provenance comment line included. The page links the tracked file by relative path (rule (b), full filename).
- **The Python block** (tag `tuned-copper-yaml`; writes only under its own `femem_out` directory; exactly one `run_study_file(` call, no `Study(` call):

  ```python
  from fem_em_solver import run_study_file

  # save the study file above as f_human_tuned_copper.yaml next to this script
  run = run_study_file("f_human_tuned_copper.yaml", output_dir="femem_out")
  ccw = run.summary["sar"]["values"]["ccw"][:18]  # the 18 lattice centres
  sar_star = max(entry["averaged_sar_w_per_kg"] for entry in ccw)
  print(f"SAR10g(c*) = {sar_star:.4e} W/kg")
  ```
  The block names adapt to the landed API only (`run.summary`, `["sar"]["values"]["ccw"]`, `averaged_sar_w_per_kg` are 390b's reads); the executor discloses any adaptation.

**What a negative result means.** Report and stop; park on `attempt/WF-17-step5-<UTC>`, mark item 402 BLOCKED in the same commit (rule (d)), journal in `docs/testing/attempts.md`. (Y=) red ⇒ the page's YAML is not the tracked file: fix the page (in-letter), re-run W0. (K) red with (Y=) green ⇒ the scratch path or `chdir` is wrong, not the loader. (R) red while 390b's W1 reproduces on the same `main` ⇒ the block differs from 390b's call — diff the two, fix the page, re-run W0 then W1 (the re-run is the window of record); a second red parks. Both red ⇒ a `main` regression: stop, known-issues entry. Solve count > 1 ⇒ a finding against the page's reads (never a re-band). (N2′) inside the band ⇒ a blind comparator: stop. (D) red ⇒ fix the link (in-letter), re-run W0b. No `PREDICTED RED:` line; a red anywhere stops the slot.

- **What you will compute:** the F2182-style gel box (and `MAT-19`'s ball) heated by a prescribed source — uniform, transverse-quadratic 3Q(y² + z²)/(B² + C²), duty-cycled — under Robin cooling, steady or Crank–Nicolson, with ball-average probes; no field solve.   ```python
  from fem_em_solver import run_study_file

  FILES = ["thermal_box_quadratic_steady_m4.yaml", "..."]  # this example's file set, next to this script
  runs = {name: run_study_file(name, output_dir=f"femem_out/{name[:-5]}") for name in FILES}
  for name, run in runs.items():
      print(name, run.summary["temperature"]["source"])
  ```
  The summary keys adapt to the landed API only (`run.summary["temperature"]` is the gates' read); disclose any adaptation. A ```` ```bash thermal-box-check ```` block shows `femem check` on the three malformed files and says each refuses with its field named.

- **What this does not show:** no EM, coil or SAR; no perfusion; P2 for the quadratic files, P1 for the others; m = 4 / m = 6 meshes only, no h-statement; the probe readings are real-build records printed beside a complex run; no CEM43 dose (P39′ / P41); no compliance statement; no AED.

**The page `docs/guide/sweep_from_yaml.md`.** Siblings: `four_port_outputs_from_yaml.md` / `tuning_from_yaml.md`. Headings: `# A frequency sweep from YAML: one mesh, one Touchstone file`, `## What you will compute`, `## Before you start`, `## The study file`, `## Run it`, `## What you should see`, `## What this does not show`, `## Worked examples`, `## How this page is tested`.
- **What you will compute:** the F-small four-port birdcage (exact port rectangles, κ off, degree 1), meshed once and solved at the frequencies listed under `study.sweep_hz`. The output is one multi-frequency Touchstone file plus the JSON summary's per-frequency S. Prose only, no number.
- **Before you start:** complex build, **2 MPI ranks**, ≈ 1 min. Copy `f_small_sweep.yaml` next to your script; link it by relative path with the full filename (rule (b)).
- **The study file:** one row for `f_small_sweep.yaml` (`study.sweep_hz`, `WF-1` step 21), saying what it sets, the summary keys it adds (`sweep_hz`, `s_matrices`, `z_matrices`, `touchstone`) and the record it carries, by name. One sentence lists what a sweep file refuses, in prose with no message text: a conductor, `as_hole`, capacitor sheets, SAR / `|B₁⁺|` / probe outputs, `tune:` and `--backend palace`.
- **Run it:** one ```` ```python four-port-sweep ```` block with **exactly one `run_study_file(` call** and no `Study(`:

  ```python
  from fem_em_solver import run_study_file

  run = run_study_file("f_small_sweep.yaml", output_dir="femem_out/f_small_sweep")
  print(run.summary["sweep_hz"], run.paths["touchstone"].name)
  ```

  It is followed by a ```` ```bash four-port-sweep-cli ```` block holding the one line `femem run f_small_sweep.yaml --output-dir femem_out/f_small_sweep`. Its text is asserted; it is never run here, because `EX-139` runs it. The summary keys adapt to the landed API only, and any adaptation is disclosed.
- **What you should see:** a ```` ```text four-port-sweep-bands ```` block with three lines, each rendered from a constant at `.0e`:
  - `S vs the P1 records, per frequency: 1e-09`;
  - `Touchstone round trip, S: 1e-12`;
  - `Touchstone round trip, frequency (Hz): 1e-03`.

  A sentence says the test, not the block, computes the readings. **No tie or round-trip digit appears on the page.**
- **What this does not show:**
  - degree 2 (`P2_X1` is a `-n 8` record);
  - copper, hole, capacitor or F-human sweeps (refused);
  - SAR / `|B₁⁺|` / probes per frequency (refused);
  - a resonance or tuning statement;

**The page `docs/guide/estimate_before_a_solve.md`.**
- Siblings: `sweep_from_yaml.md`, `four_port_outputs_from_yaml.md`.
- Headings: `# Estimating memory and time before a solve`, `## What you will compute`, `## Before you start`, `## Run it`, `## What you should see`, `## What the estimate is built from`, `## What this does not show`, `## Worked examples`, `## How this page is tested`.
- **What you will compute:** a mesh and an unknown count for the study file, plus a predicted peak memory and wall time at the current launch width. Nothing is solved. Prose only, no number.
- **Before you start:** complex build, **2 MPI ranks**, ≈ 1 min (the mesh is built, the solve is not). Copy `f_small_four_port.yaml` next to the script and link it by relative path with the full filename (rule (b)).
- **Run it:**
  - One ```` ```python estimate-python ```` block with **exactly one `load_study(` call, one `.estimate(` call and no `.solve(` / `run_study_file(`**:

    ```python
    from mpi4py import MPI
    from fem_em_solver import load_study

    study = load_study("f_small_four_port.yaml")
    est = study.estimate(MPI.COMM_WORLD)
    print(est.cells, est.unknowns, est.degree, est.ranks)
    ```
  - Then one ```` ```bash estimate-cli ```` block with the one line `mpiexec -n 2 femem run --estimate f_small_four_port.yaml`.
- **What you should see:** a ```` ```text estimate-fields ```` block listing the field names one per line in dataclass order: `cells`, `unknowns`, `degree`, `ranks`, `predicted_peak_gib`, `predicted_seconds`, `rows_used`, `not_priced`. It is asserted equal to `[f.name for f in dataclasses.fields(ResourceEstimate)]`. A sentence says the CLI prints one `name: value` line per field on rank 0. **No reading on the page**: no cell count, unknown count, GiB or seconds (the records are the test's).
- **What the estimate is built from:** prose. A power law per element degree is fitted to measured solves (memory against unknowns, time × ranks against unknowns). A study at a degree with fewer than two measured rows is refused by name. A thermal-only study is refused. `--ranks` with `--estimate` is refused, because the estimate prices the current launch width. No row digit, no table.

- **What you will compute:** prose only. The transient temperature rise of a Robin-cooled gel ball heated by a uniform source switched on and off by a duty cycle (Crank–Nicolson with Rannacher start-up). Then the same ball inside a container wall of different conductivity and heat capacity. No number.
- **Before you start:** the **real** build (no `dolfinx-complex-mode`; thermal-only studies need no complex solve), 2 MPI ranks, ≈ 1 min per block.
- **A duty-cycled source:** one ```` ```python duty-python ```` block. It holds exactly one `Study(` call, one `.temperature(` call with the keywords `transient=` and `duty=`, and no `.solve(` / `run_study_file(`. In the shape of `_duty_study` (`test_wf15_study_transient.py:175–179`), it builds:
  - `ThermalBallGeometry(<R>, <RES>)`;
  - `UniformHeatSource(<Q>)`;
  - `RobinCooling(<K>, <H>, <T_INF>)`;
  - `TransientSpec(150.0, 94, <RHO_C>)`;
  - `DutyCycle(<P_A>, <D_A>)`.

- **What you will compute:** prose only, no number. The F-small four-port birdcage with its rings and legs cut out of the mesh (`as_hole: true`). The cut surfaces carry a Leontovich surface impedance for copper. It is solved at 64 MHz, one port at a time, and the output is the 4×4 S-matrix in a Touchstone file plus the JSON summary.

The record it carries is `WF-1` step 4: the S class means reproduce `ANS-6`'s degree-1 copper record.   ```python
  from fem_em_solver import (CapacitorSheets, FourPortBirdcageGeometry,
                             LeontovichWall, Study)
  from fem_em_solver.materials import GelledSalinePhantomMaterial

  study = Study(
      FourPortBirdcageGeometry(as_hole=True, strip_fraction=0.5),
      GelledSalinePhantomMaterial(0.5, 78.0, 64.0e6),
      CapacitorSheets(6.629e-11, esr_ohm=0.2, on=("P2",)),
      frequency_hz=64.0e6,
      degree=1,
      conductor=LeontovichWall(5.8e7),
  )
  result = study.solve()
  print([p.port_id for p in result.port_definitions], result.capacitor_impedance_ohm)
  ```

  Each literal is copied from the gate constants (`SALINE_SIGMA`, `SALINE_EPSILON_R`, `FREQUENCY_64_HZ`, `GATED_WIDTH_FRACTION`, `C_F`, `ESR_OHM`, `COPPER_SIGMA`). - **Why every capacitor carries 0.2 Ω:** prose from the binding ruling, with no AED value. Real RF capacitors are lossy; a lossless C overstates the Q of a copper coil, whose own conductor loss is small enough that the capacitor's ESR dominates it. The project puts r = 0.2 Ω on every capacitor. A uniform r adds r to every mode's eigen-impedance, so `C_mode` is unchanged. No ruling text beyond that, no HFSS mapping (the R_p conversion is AED-side and belongs to the SPECs).

Readings: (R-S) 4.624947e-12 (prior 4.383149e-12), (R-Y) ccw 2.834e-12 / cw 3.103e-12 (prior 2.446e-12 / 3.160e-12), (R-SAR) max rel 2.857e-10 (prior equal), all ≤ 1e-06. **Note for the next rerun:** W-B5 wrote `logs/wf17/s5/femem_out` again, so the next W0 rerun meets the same premise and must clear it first. §10 U149 DONE; `WF-17` stays 🟡 on item 943's hold, with step 40 added as a record.

**Review ruling (2026-11-10 10:30 daily — what "every record at its prior" means; binds items 938–948 over their briefs; provisional, the 11-11 weekly ratifies or reverts).** Items 938, 939 and 940 stopped BLOCKED on values that moved at round-off with every band green. The `log-pathologist` ruling (journal `docs/planning/reviews/2026-11-10-daily-1030.md` §4) classed every moved value as a residual or a distance-to-record, checked only against a band. No asserted-equality value and no fixed-precision record print moved in any of the three windows. U146's own closure (item 928) set the precedent: (IT), (Pr) and (B) moved at round-off and the item closed DONE. So, for chain U's re-run steps:
- **A record** is (a) a value the node asserts by equality (`== *_PRINT`, string or list equality, `rel 0`), or (b) a named fixed-precision print of a physical quantity at **11 significant digits or fewer**: a `.4e` / `.6e` / `.10e` value, a `.4f` list, a cell or dof count, a schedule string, a sha256. A record off its prior is BLOCKED, as the briefs say.
- **A reading** is an identity residual (reciprocity, (U′), (Δ), (IT), (E), a node-JSON residual) or a distance-to-record (`rel …`, `vs … record`, (R) / (R4) / (R′)). So is any print finer than 11 significant digits that the node does not assert by equality: `repr`, `.11e` and finer, `.12f` near 1, hex (for example (c4)'s 8.291558128523365e-08, σ_max 0.999851545099, a `.12e` C_mode, S31's θ(P5) +1.224895450933e+00). Round-off at 1e-13 relative reaches their last digits. It is printed beside its prior and gated by its band only. When it moves inside its band, it is written in the Outcome and **the slot continues**: no known-issues entry, no 🟡.
- **One unit in the last printed place.** A (b) record that moves by exactly one unit in its last printed digit, with its band green, is read as a rounding-boundary crossing. It is printed beside its prior, the slot continues, and the review reads it. Two or more units is BLOCKED.
- A band red is BLOCKED, as before.

The known-issues entries of items 938, 939 and 940 are retired by this ruling. `WF-17`'s hold is lifted and the row returns to ✅. Items 943–945 run the windows the three items did not reach. Where a brief below this ruling lists a value as a record that this ruling classes as a reading, the ruling binds. The same goes for 941, 942 and 948, whose briefs predate it or were drafted beside it.

Ratified by the 2026-11-11 weekly (PROJECT_PLAN §10 rulings 2026-11-11 (a), (b)); standing.

- **For the review.** The tracked logs hold 11 distinct `.10e` prints of the degree-2 sphere SAR, from 8.0116577233e-08 to 8.0116577256e-08 (≈ 2.9e-11 relative). 8.0116577245e-08 appears 20 times. Mesh, width and route were not checked per log. So the degree-2 `.10e` print does not hold steady at its eleventh digit. If the review classes it as a reading, step 39 closes on these windows.

**Review ruling (2026-11-10 18:00 daily — a solve output's print and its named constant; binds over item 943's brief and every later chain U re-run brief; provisional, the 11-11 weekly ratifies or reverts).** A `log-pathologist` read item 943's windows (journal `docs/planning/reviews/2026-11-10-daily-1800.md` §4). All of them are COUNTABLE.
- **(R) lands on its record.** The node's named record is `WF15_DEG2_RECORD = 8.0116577245e-08` (`tests/package/test_wf17_first_study_tutorial.py:72`). The node asserts SAR against it only through `rel_record <= L0_BAND` (`:340`). W-first prints `8.011657724500328e-08 (8.0116577245e-08); record 8.0116577245e-08; rel 4.097e-14` (`20261110T193150Z_WF-17-s39-W-first.log:138`), which equals the constant at every printed digit. The brief's prior, 8.0116577252e-08 (`20261010T124220Z_OPS-70-S2-W7-wf17-first-study-deg2.log.gz:162`), was itself 8.553e-11 off that constant. **The 10:30 review's own brief listed that print as the record. That was this review series' error, not the slot's.**
- **The degree-2 print is not reproducible at its 11th digit.** Pairs on the same commit, route, mesh (74020 cells) and `-n 2` differ by 4–10 units in the `.10e` last place. Examples:
  - `20261006T202424Z_EX-91.log.gz:188` reads …7237, against `20261006T202928Z_EX-91-control.log.gz:181` …7246;
  - `20261007T110359Z_EX-96.log.gz:263` reads …7233, against `20261007T110900Z_EX-96-control.log.gz:256` …7238.
  
  The spread reaches 1.5e-10 relative, and one unit is ≈ 1.25e-11. The pathologist's caveat is that in each such pair one run sets `FEM_EM_SETUP_FIGURES=1`, so a figure-flag effect is not excluded. Only 3 of the "20 occurrences" of …7245 are computed values; the rest echo the constant.
- **Correction to item 943's Outcome:** W-rep and W-four differ by 1.65e-20 absolute, which is **1.99e-12 relative**, not ≈ 2e-13 (`…W-four-rep.log:1919` against `…W-four.log:1919`; the distances to `MAT4_DEG1_RECORD`, 6.113e-11 and 6.312e-11, differ by the same amount). Both ran on the same `src/` and `tests/` (`eec9179e` → `2977d6b4` adds only `test_geo36_i88_…`, which neither command collects). The degree-1 solve is therefore nondeterministic run to run at ≈ 2e-12.
- **So, refining the 10:30 ruling's (b):** where a node names a module constant for a solve output and gates it by a band, **the constant is the record and the print is a reading of it.** The print is gated by that band and printed beside both the constant and the last run's print. A (b) record remains a print with no named constant, such as a count, a sha, a `.4e` list or an asserted string. The 1-unit / 2-unit rule still binds those.
- **Consequences:**
  - Item 943 is DONE on W-rep and W-first, and step 39 is a record (with item 938's W0-a / W0-b / W-four).
  - §10 U148 is DONE.
  - Known-issues 2026-11-10 (item 943) is RETIRED.
  - `WF-17` returns to ✅. It is not re-audited, because the glyph returns on a ruling as at 10:30.
- **Measurement owed, not queued:** two back-to-back W-first runs on one clean commit with an identical command, printing `repr`, would settle the figure-flag confound. No status waits on it.
- No band, tolerance, done-when or target moved.

Ratified by the 2026-11-11 weekly (PROJECT_PLAN §10 rulings 2026-11-11 (a), (b)); standing.

- **Killed at the ceiling:** report, `pgrep -c python3`, stop; never raise it.

- No `PREDICTED RED:` line.

1. `mkdocs build --strict` cannot exit 0: `docs/guide/` alone carries ≈ 1 540 links to files outside `docs/` (`../../src/…`, `../../tests/…`), each a WARNING under mkdocs 1.6; downgrading them with a `validation:` block would loosen the assertion, and an `xfail` would not assert it.
2. `mkdocs.yml` has no `exclude_docs`, so a build copies the gitignored `docs/private/` and `docs/references/` (the operator-provided Jin text) into `site/`. **No slot runs a docs build until that is excluded.**
3. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` already carries a `docs` job that builds `--strict` and deploys with `peaceiris/actions-gh-pages@v3`, so U165 as posed would be a second deployer.
4. `pip install ".[docs]"` pulls the core dependencies (dolfinx) as well, so "installs only the docs extra" needs the extra read out of `pyproject.toml`.
5. `check_private_leak.py` scans staged changes, a message or the tracked tree, not a built site.

Operator item: the repository's Pages source must be set to "GitHub Actions" (no slot can). The weekly rewrites the two anchors — the link policy, the single deployer, the exclusions; the drafts below are execution detail it may reuse. The image does carry the toolchain (mkdocs 1.6.1, material, mkdocstrings, PyYAML 6.0.3 import in the container, drafter's check).

### §9 item 994 — 2026-11-15 (§10 U164, chain U — rule (4), the 11-14 weekly's step; ops)

*The 11-14 weekly's own enumerated step (rule (4); PROJECT_PLAN.md:5264–5273), executing the operator directive of 2026-11-14 (a) (PROJECT_PLAN.md:4721–4728, commit `7c2b7bc2`: docs on GitHub Pages, "no AED value or private file ever enters the built site"). `WF-17` step N (next free step number; read it from the tail of `docs/planning/chunks/WF-17.md`). Serial on nothing. No solve, no mesh, `-n 1`.*

**Anchor.** The three assertions of the weekly text: (B) `python3 -m mkdocs build --strict` exits 0 with zero `WARNING` lines; (P) the built site carries no path containing `aed_results`, `COMPARISON_private` or `private/` (the `docs/private` tree, which builds to `<site>/private/`); (L) the site's text passes `check_private_leak.py`'s digit scan against the raw AED exports. The build time is printed as a price and never asserted.

**PREDICTED RED: (B), and (P) on the tree as it stands.** Mechanism, verified 2026-11-15:
- **(P) red as is.** `mkdocs.yml` (25 lines) sets no `docs_dir` and no `exclude_docs`, so the docs directory is all of `docs/`. MkDocs copies every file under it into the site, whether or not the file is in `nav`. On this host `docs/private/` holds the gitignored adjudication files (`.gitignore:139–140`; `ls docs/private` shows ten-plus files), and `docs/references/` holds the operator-provided Jin text, both as markdown (`jin-fem-3e/`, `.gitignore:128`) and as an `.epub`. A plain build therefore publishes the private files *and* a copyrighted book.
- **(B) red as is.** mkdocs is 1.6.1 in the image (measured: `python3 -c "import mkdocs; print(mkdocs.__version__)"` in the container prints `1.6.1`; PyYAML `6.0.3`). In 1.6, `validation.links.not_found` defaults to `warn`. `docs/guide/` alone carries ≈1 540 relative links of the form `](../../src|scripts|tests|examples/…)` that point outside `docs/`, and `docs/` as a whole carries ≈1 545 `](../…)` links (grep count, 2026-11-15). Each such link is a `WARNING`, so `--strict` aborts. What this step can do: fix (P) mechanically and **measure** (B).

**Negative control (asserted).** The control runs in `tmp_path` on a synthetic docs tree, never on the repo:
- (c-P): a scratch `docs/` with `index.md` and `private/x.md`, plus a scratch `mkdocs.yml` *without* `exclude_docs`. Build it, and the (P) checker must find `private/` in the site. Then add the repo's `exclude_docs` block, rebuild, and the checker must find nothing.
- (c-L): one line holding a digit string generated at run time (`secrets.randbelow`, ≥ 10 digits, ≥ 5 distinct, never written to a tracked file), passed with `{that string}` as the secret set to `check_private_leak.scan`. It must return exactly one hit. Then the same scan over the real site lines with the real secret set.
- (c-B): a scratch page linking `../outside.py` under `--strict` must exit non-zero and print a `WARNING` line, which shows the warning counter can count.

**Tier / ranks / cost.** Standard (180 s ceiling), `-n 1` (no MPI; a build is rank-free). Real or complex image mode does not matter. The build reads 632 `.md` files, including the 1.8 MB `docs/testing/test-results.md`. Prediction: 30–120 s per build, three builds (repo plus the two scratch controls; the scratch builds are seconds). **Class:** ops (median ≈ 5.1 min). **Cost:** ≈ 8 predicted min.

**Traps.**
- **Never build into `site/` in the repo.** Use `--site-dir <tmp_path>/site`. `site/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:47`), but a built site holding `private/` must never sit in the work tree, even an ignored one.
- **`exclude_docs` in 1.6 replaces the default exclusions.** The default is `.*` and `/templates/`. Restate both in the block: `.*`, `/templates/`, `private/`, `references/`. Prove it with the (c-P) control, not by reading docs.
- **Excluding `references/` is a privacy and copyright exclusion, not a nav choice.** `docs/references/README.md` and `birdcage-tuning-literature.md` are tracked but go too: the whole directory is excluded so that the gitignored subtree can never slip through.
- `check_private_leak.py` (`scripts/testing/`, 419 lines) **has no scan-a-directory mode**. Its CLI modes are staged, `--message-file` and `--audit` (`:392–405`). Import it by path (`importlib.util.spec_from_file_location`), and feed `scan(lines, secrets)` (`:310–334`) `(relpath, lineno, text)` triples from every text file in the site (`.html`, `.js`, `.json`, `.xml`, `.txt`; `search/search_index.json` holds the full text). Use `secret_digits()` (`:190–194`) **without** `published_digits()` subtraction (audit semantics: the site is a publication). Report the exit-code meanings of `:24–31`.
- **An empty secret set is not a pass** (`:339–343` prints `SKIPPED`). In the container the exports exist: `examples/ansys_benchmarks/*/aed_results` lists 8 directories on this host. Assert `len(secrets) > 0` and print the count. Print hit **locations only, never the token** (`:381–383`); this output lands in a tracked log.
- **Count `WARNING` lines from the build's combined stdout+stderr** (`subprocess.run(..., capture_output=True, text=True)`), with the regex `^WARNING\b` per line. In `--strict`, mkdocs 1.6 prints the warnings and then `Aborted with N warnings in strict mode!`. Record N and classify the first 20 by message type (link-not-found / nav / other). Never print a private page's content.
- `docs/guide/api/` pages carry no `:::` mkdocstrings directives (grep 2026-11-15), and `mkdocs.yml` enables only `search`. Do not add `mkdocstrings` to `plugins`: it would import `femem`, and that is out of scope.
- `scripts/build_docs.sh` runs a bare `mkdocs build` into `site/`. Leave it untouched, and do not use it.

**Scope.** Edits allowed:
- **`mkdocs.yml`**: the `exclude_docs` block only.
- **The new test module** `tests/package/test_wf17_docs_build.py`.
- **Plan annotations**: the WF-17 chunk file outcome, §10 U164 and the §9 row.

**Not licensed:**
- any `validation:` block or other downgrade of a warning to info. That is loosening the (B) assertion; hard rule.
- any rewrite of the ≈1 540 outbound links, or any generator edit.
- any `nav` change.
- `site_url`, which is U165's.
- any `.github/` edit.

**Not claimed:** a hosted site, a published URL, a nav that covers the guide.

**Negative result.**
- **(B) red after the exclusion, as predicted.** The step's result is the measured warning census: N in total, the per-class counts, and the first 20 lines (paths only). Land the `exclude_docs` edit and the test module with (P), (L) and the three controls asserted. Mark (B) as `pytest.mark.xfail(strict=True, reason="U164: N strict warnings, link-not-found; re-posed to the weekly")`. A strict xfail flips red the day the warnings reach 0, so it is not a loosening. Write it up in known-issues. The re-pose goes to the 11-16 weekly, and the operator has to choose:
  - rewrite outbound links to absolute `repo_url` blob links;
  - narrow `docs_dir` to a site-only tree;
  - or rule a `validation` policy.
- **If the review reads the xfail as a loosening,** park the test on `attempt/*` instead and land only the measurement.
- **(P) or (L) red after the exclusion** ⇒ stop, file it in known-issues, commit nothing that builds a site.
- **`mkdocs` not importable** ⇒ that finding is the result, not a red. It is an operator item: the docs extra in the image. Measured 2026-11-15, `mkdocs` imports, so this branch is not expected.

**Status it can move.** `WF-17` (✅) gains step N as a record. §10 U164 DONE, either green on all of (B)/(P)/(L) or DONE-with-negative-result as above. The directive's "no private file enters the site" becomes a mechanism.

**Facts verified (2026-11-15).**
- `docker/Dockerfile:22–24`: `mkdocs`, `mkdocs-material`, `"mkdocstrings[python]"` (`pyyaml` at `:15`). The container imports `mkdocs`, `material`, `mkdocstrings` and `yaml`, at mkdocs `1.6.1` and PyYAML `6.0.3`.

### §9 item 995 — 2026-11-15 (§10 U165, chain U — rule (4), the 11-14 weekly's step; ops)

*The 11-14 weekly's own enumerated step (rule (4); PROJECT_PLAN.md:5274–5281), executing the operator directive of 2026-11-14 (a) (PROJECT_PLAN.md:4721–4728, commit `7c2b7bc2`). `WF-17` step N+1 (or N if item 994 has not landed; read the chunk file tail). Serial on nothing. No solve. **A slot never pushes.** The publish waits on the operator's manual push, and origin is stale.*

**Anchor.** The weekly's four assertions, in a test that reads only files and runs offline in the container:
- (Y) `.github/workflows/docs.yml` parses with `yaml.safe_load`.
- (T) it triggers on `push` to `main` only: `branches == ["main"]`, and no `pull_request`, `schedule`, `workflow_dispatch` or tag trigger.
- (I) the install step installs only the `docs` extra.
- (X) no file under `.github/workflows/` mentions `aed_results`, `COMPARISON_private`, `docs/private` or `private/`.

Plus the config half of the step:
- (S) `mkdocs.yml`'s `site_url` is `https://tayebzaidi.github.io/precessem/`. That URL is derived from `repo_url: https://github.com/tayebzaidi/precessem` (`mkdocs.yml:3`).
- (A) the deploy uses only official actions: `actions/checkout`, `actions/setup-python`, `actions/configure-pages`, `actions/upload-pages-artifact` and `actions/deploy-pages`. Every `uses:` matches `^actions/`.
- (D) exactly one Pages deployer exists across `.github/workflows/`.

**Negative control (asserted).** Every checker runs on three in-memory YAML strings that each break one rule. Each must be refused by exactly its own checker:
- one with `pull_request:` added (T);
- one with `pip install ".[docs,dev]"` (I);
- one with `uses: peaceiris/actions-gh-pages@v3` (A, and D when merged with the real file set).

A fourth string, with a `docs/private/x` path in a `run:` line, must trip (X). The real `docs.yml` must pass all of them.

**Tier / ranks / cost.** Smoke (30 s ceiling), `-n 1`, no MPI, no build. **Class:** ops (median ≈ 5.1 min). **Cost:** ≈ 6 predicted min.

**Traps.**
- **YAML 1.1 parses the bare key `on:` as boolean `True`** under PyYAML 6.0.3 (in the image; `docker/Dockerfile:15`). Read the triggers as `doc.get("on", doc.get(True))`, and assert that exactly one of the two keys exists. Never quote `on` in the workflow just to please the test: GitHub accepts both forms, and the test must handle the canonical one.
- **"Installs only the `docs` extra" cannot be `pip install ".[docs]"`.** That installs the package itself and its core dependencies (`pyproject.toml:25–39`: `fenics-dolfinx>=0.11.0,<0.12`, `petsc4py`, `slepc4py`, `gmsh`, …), which are not pip-installable on an `ubuntu-latest` runner. Read the extra from `pyproject.toml` instead:
  ```
  python -c "import tomllib; print('\n'.join(tomllib.load(open('pyproject.toml','rb'))['project']['optional-dependencies']['docs']))" > docs-requirements.txt
  pip install -r docs-requirements.txt
  ```
  `tomllib` needs Python ≥ 3.11, so pin `python-version: '3.11'` or later. Test (I) as follows:
  - exactly one step runs `pip install`, and it is `-r` on the file generated from `optional-dependencies']['docs']`;
  - no other `pip`, `conda` or `apt` install step exists.

  Also assert from `pyproject.toml` (parsed with `tomllib` in the container) that the `docs` extra is exactly the three pins at `:57–61`, so a widened extra is caught.
- **A Pages deployer already exists.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:363–385` has a `docs` job: `pip install "mkdocs<2" mkdocs-material "mkdocstrings[python]"` (not the extra), then `mkdocs build --strict`, then `peaceiris/actions-gh-pages@v3` (third-party, pushing a `gh-pages` branch) when `github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'`. That job is triggered by ci.yml's `push: [main, develop]` and `pull_request: [main]` (`ci.yml:3–8`). Two deployers race, and the official route needs the repository's Pages source set to "GitHub Actions" rather than a branch.
  - **Remove the `docs` job from `ci.yml`** (lines 363–385 only, nothing else in that file). That is the single licensed `ci.yml` edit, and (D) asserts it.
  - **Operator item:** the Pages source setting ("GitHub Actions") is a repository setting no slot can change. It goes on the dashboard's Waiting-on-you list beside the manual push.
- The official deploy needs:
  - `permissions: {contents: read, pages: write, id-token: write}`;
  - `concurrency: {group: pages, cancel-in-progress: false}`;
  - a `deploy` job with `environment: {name: github-pages, url: ${{ steps.deployment.outputs.page_url }}}` and `needs: build`;
  - `upload-pages-artifact` `path: site`.

  Assert the permissions and the environment name in (A). The action major versions are not verifiable offline: state the ones used (`checkout@v4`, `setup-python@v5`, `configure-pages@v5`, `upload-pages-artifact@v3`, `deploy-pages@v4`), and do not assert them.
- **The build step runs `mkdocs build --strict`,** the same command as U164. It will be red on the runner for as long as U164's (B) is red, because the ≈1 540 outbound `../../src…` links warn. This step does not fix that and does not drop `--strict`, which would be a loosening. The runner's red, if a push happens first, is the U164 finding seen remotely.
- **On a runner, `docs/private/*` and `docs/references/jin-fem-3e/` are absent** (gitignored). The `exclude_docs` from item 994 is defence in depth there, not the only net.
- **The guard denies shell text that is not a bare harness call.** Edit the YAML with Edit/Write only, and never `printf` or redirect from Bash.

**Scope.** Edits allowed:
- **New** `.github/workflows/docs.yml`.
- **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`**: the `docs` job removed (`:363–385`), nothing else.
- **`mkdocs.yml`**: `site_url` added, nothing else.
- **The new test module** `tests/package/test_wf17_pages_workflow.py`.
- **Plan annotations.**

**Not licensed:**
- any change to other `ci.yml` jobs;
- `workflow_dispatch`;
- a push, a tag, or a `gh` call;
- touching `nav`, `exclude_docs`, links or `pyproject.toml`.

**Not claimed:** a published site, a green runner, the Pages repository setting.

**Negative result.**
- **PyYAML or `tomllib` not importable in the container** ⇒ stop. It is an image item and goes to known-issues. Measured 2026-11-15: PyYAML imports. `tomllib` is stdlib on the image's Python; confirm it in W0.
- **Any checker fails its own negative control** ⇒ the checker is wrong. Fix the checker, never the control.

**Status it can move.**
- `WF-17` (✅) gains a step as a record, and §10 U165 is DONE.
- The dashboard's Waiting-on-you list gains two items: "set Pages source to GitHub Actions" and "manual push".
- v0's docs-hosting item is configured but not published.

**Facts verified (2026-11-15).**
- `.github/` exists; `workflows/` holds only `ci.yml`. Its `docs` job is at `:363–385`, and its triggers at `:3–8` are push to `main`/`develop` and pull_request to `main`.
- `mkdocs.yml` has no `site_url`; `repo_url` is at `:3`.
- `pyproject.toml:57–61`: the `docs` extra is three pins. `:25–39`: the core dependencies include `fenics-dolfinx`.
- The container has PyYAML `6.0.3` and mkdocs `1.6.1`. `docker/Dockerfile:15` installs `pyyaml`; `:22–24` installs the mkdocs toolchain.

**Files.**
- New: `.github/workflows/docs.yml`, `tests/package/test_wf17_pages_workflow.py`.
- Edited: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (one job removed), `mkdocs.yml` (`site_url`).
- New logs: `docs/testing/logs/*_WF-17-u165-*.log`, plus `test-results.md` rows.
- Plan: the WF-17 chunk outcome, §10 U165 and the §9 row; the dashboard items go to the review.

**Windows** (bare, whole Bash command, foreground, Bash `timeout: 120000`):
1. **W0.** The test module before `docs.yml` exists. Expected: (Y) red with file absent, and (D) red on the peaceiris job (the pre-step control, disclosed). Also run `python3 -c 'import tomllib'`.
   `scripts/testing/run_and_log.sh WF-17-u165-W0 "docker compose exec -T fem-em-solver bash -lc 'cd /workspace && PYTHONPATH=/workspace/src timeout -k 30 30 python3 -m pytest tests/package/test_wf17_pages_workflow.py -v --tb=short -s'"`
2. **W1.** Same command, label `WF-17-u165-W1`, after `docs.yml`, the `ci.yml` job removal and `site_url`. Expected: all green, controls included.

**Depends on.** Nothing (serial on nothing, per the weekly). If item 994 lands first, its `exclude_docs` block sits beside `site_url` in `mkdocs.yml`. The two edits do not overlap.

### §9 item 997 — 2026-11-15 (ops; known-issues 2026-11-15)

*Ops item queued by the 2026-11-15 18:00 daily review. **Review ruling:** the item may edit the two `MAT-21` modules and the closed-form page module as ops (one cause, one fix; no `MAT-21` record or band moves); a pointer is added to `MAT-21.md`. Exempting ledger-cited logs from gzip in `housekeeping.py` is not this item's; it goes to the 11-16 weekly. It resolves the known-issues entry "2026-11-15 — 🟡 OPEN (`WF-17` U163, §9 item 992 …)" (`docs/testing/known-issues.md:905–911`), which blocks item 992. The fix is a test-side reader fix: no solve, no `src/` edit, no retention-policy edit. **Deadline:** land before the next weekly sweep, Sunday **2026-11-22 01:45 CST (07:45 UTC)** (`scripts/automation/crontab:35`). Otherwise eight more landed nodes go red the same way (table below).*

**Why.** `scripts/maintenance/housekeeping.py` gzips **every** tracked log once it is 7 days old, cited or not (`COMPRESS_DAYS = 7`, `plan_logs` `:138–143`). Citation, through the `CITATION` regex `:80` over `CITATION_SOURCES` / `CITATION_GLOBS` `:44–63` plus `retention-keep.txt`, exempts a log from **deletion** at `DELETE_DAYS = 14` (`:140`), never from gzip. `tests/` is not a citation source. A test that opens `docs/testing/logs/<name>.log` as plain text therefore breaks on the first sweep after its log turns 7 days old. `OPS-73` already wrote the shared fix: `tests/log_records.py` provides `resolve_log_path` / `read_log_text` / `glob_log_paths`, tested by `tests/unit/test_log_records.py`. **This item uses that helper. It writes no new helper and does not edit `tests/log_records.py`.** `test_wf13_estimate_f_clinical_d2.py:47–52` uses the same pattern inline.

**Sweep of plain-text readers (measured 2026-11-15 on `7db96c4c`).** Method: every full log basename in a non-docstring string literal under `tests/`, plus ledger-derived names, cross-checked against each module's read sites. Modules already routed through `log_records`, `gzip.open` or a local `.gz`-aware `_read_log` are excluded. The `examples/*` `_read_log` readers that tests load are `.gz`-aware (`ans11 …py:135–141`).

If the plain form exists, write `gzip.compress(plain.read_bytes())` to `tmp_path/<name>.log.gz`, with no plain file in `tmp_path`. If only `.gz` exists, write `gzip.decompress(gz.read_bytes())` to `tmp_path/<name>.log`. Then assert `read_log_text(tmp/<name>.log, errors="strict") == read_log_text(LOGS/<name>.log, errors="strict")`, and assert that both equal `io.TextIOWrapper(io.BytesIO(raw), encoding="utf-8", newline=None).read()` of the raw bytes, which is exactly what `Path.read_text` does.

Likewise for the 3 T entries, giving `{"58": [], "60": [], "61": []}`. The tmp dir holds each log in the **opposite** form to the one on disk.

Then assert the parse equals the parse on the real path: `_parse_c52()` tuples, `read_one(…, STATUS_RE)` and `expected()` dicts, `_c39()` dict. This proves the 11-22 state green without touching disk.
- (L) precondition, asserted and printed: `locale.getpreferredencoding(False)` reads `UTF-8` in the container. `read_log_text` reads the plain form with no `encoding=`, while the old `_custody` passed `encoding="utf-8"`.

**Negative controls (asserted).**
- (N-absent) `_custody([{"entry": "x", "log": "20990101T000000Z_ABSENT.log", "seconds": "1", "gib": "1.00"}])` raises `FileNotFoundError`, and its message names both `…ABSENT.log` and `…ABSENT.log.gz`. A missing record is never read as empty.
- (N-trunc) a tmp copy of entry 48's real `.gz`, truncated to half its bytes, raises when passed to `_custody(…, logs=tmp)`. Expect `EOFError` ("Compressed file ended before the end-of-stream marker…"). Assert `pytest.raises((EOFError, OSError, zlib.error))` and print the class. `errors="strict"`/`"replace"` governs only text decoding, never the gzip stream, so this must raise whatever `errors` is.
- (N-crc) the same copy with one byte of the CRC32 trailer flipped (bytes `-8…-5`) raises `gzip.BadGzipFile` ("CRC check failed"). A corrupted record is not silently accepted.
- (N-digit) the existing `c-52` / `c-61` / `c-ledger` nodes stay green. `c-52` now runs custody **through the `.gz` path** (entry 52), so a one-digit seconds change still fails custody on that entry alone. The fallback has not neutered custody.
- (N-pre) W0 below is the pre-edit control: the bare reader on the gz-only state raises `FileNotFoundError` (and `test_log_records.py::test_nc_bare_read_fails_on_gz_only` stays green).

**Traps already paid for.**
- **Never gunzip a record to get green.** Do not decompress, copy back, re-add or `git checkout` a plain `.log` into `docs/testing/logs/`. With both forms present, `resolve_log_path` prefers the plain one (`test_h_plain_wins_when_both_exist`), so a hand-gunzipped file would mask the bug until the next sweep. All opposite-form copies live in `tmp_path` only.
- **Never edit a ledger row** (`xl-ledger.md`, `xxl-ledger.md`) and never move or rename a log. The planted-ledger controls write tmp copies through `_ledger_with` only.
- Do not edit `scripts/maintenance/housekeeping.py`, `docs/testing/retention-policy.md` or `retention-keep.txt`. Exempting cited logs from gzip is a review / operator ruling, not this item's, and a keep-file pin does not stop gzip anyway (`:102–106` adds it to `cited`, which gates deletion only).
- `read_log_text`'s default is `errors="replace"`. Pass `errors="strict"` at every edited site, because the old readers decoded strictly, and a replace-mode reader would silently accept invalid UTF-8 in a custody read.
- `str.splitlines()` splits on `\x0c`, `\x1c–\x1e`, `\x85`, ` ` as well as `\n`. File iteration does not (universal newlines only). Harness logs carry gmsh / PETSc output. Where the old code iterated a file handle (`_parse_c52`), iterate `io.StringIO(text)` so the line set stays byte-identical. Where the old code already called `.splitlines()`, keep it.
- **Module hold** (PROJECT_PLAN.md:3981–3986: a queued window's module, its import closure and its module's importers are not touched before it runs). Measured at this review by AST import closure over `tests/`: `xl` entry 65 (`xl-queue.d/20261116-GEO-38-C35w.env`, `test_geo38_f_clinical_matrix.py`, 55-module closure) and `xxl` entry 69 (`xxl-queue.d/20261128-GEO-38-P33-d2.env`, `test_geo38_f_clinical_box_heating.py`, 116-module closure). Neither closure contains any of the five files above or `tests/log_records.py`, and none of them is an importer of either window module. **Re-check at slot start** against the queue directories as they stand then, since the review may have queued more. If a newly queued window's closure contains an edited module, stop and journal it. Do not edit around the hold.
- The harness `timeout` takes **`-k 30`**. Use the bare whole-command harness form: no `cd … &&`, no pipe, no redirect (`bash_guard.py`). The Bash tool runs foreground with `timeout: 660000`. Do not use `-k a or b` selection inside the quoted command, which splits into stray argv. Run whole modules.
- **Count verdicts per rank by token.** At `-n 2` each node prints `PASSED` / `FAILED` once per rank. W1 needs every collected node at 2 × `PASSED` and 0 × `FAILED` / `ERROR`. The `N passed` summary line appears twice.

**The weekly's either/or, decided: `Study.estimate` does not carry a 3 T degree-2 price. No new src row is pinned.**
- U118's branch is `_estimate_f_clinical`'s 3 T arm (`src/fem_em_solver/study.py:2595–2599`, table `PRICE_TABLE_CLINICAL_3T` `:2546–2549`), and it holds FC3 alone (degree 1). At degree 2 there is no matching row, so it returns `rows_used=()`, NaN for peak and seconds, and `not_priced=("degree 2 (no measured F-clinical row)", "thermal transient")` (`:2608–2616`). The table's own comment says the same: "FC4 (3 T, degree 2) has **no** row: `not_priced`" (`:2543–2545`).
- **A new pinned FC4 row is declined.** There are three reasons. (i) U118's landed node pins FC4 as not priced (`tests/package/test_wf13_estimate_f_clinical_3t.py:104–111`) and unpacks exactly one 3 T row (`:82`, `(row,) = PRICE_TABLE_CLINICAL_3T`). Any src row turns both red, and the 1-unit rule keeps every record at its prior. (ii) The only measured 3 T degree-2 window is entry 64 itself. An FC4 taken from it in FC2's convention (Z50 pair 1894.7 s, `memory.peak` 377.12 GiB, `:13010`, `:13067–13068`) would set a GiB ratio of 1.0000 *by construction*, which is a circular record. (iii) A `PRICE_TABLE*` change is not in the scope of a daily step. FC2 needed a different window (entry 50) before it could be compared with entry 62.
- **So the estimator row is FC4 exactly as `Study.estimate` returns it**, through a new tracked fixture, with its ratios printed as `nan`. **FC3 is printed beside it** on the same r1 mesh (cells 1 057 895 = `F_CLINICAL_3T_R1_CELL_RECORD`, `study.py:612`) at the same width (`-n 16`). FC3 / entry 64 is the only finite ratio the tree supports: the degree-1 estimator over the degree-2 measurement on one mesh at one width. *The review may drop the FC3 row; the step then prints only `nan` ratios.*
